SUPERVISED MACHINE LEARNING

Supervised learning is the Machine Learning technique to get information (pattern, features) from labeled training data (Rieke, 1997). Typically, the training data includes a set of labeled/ known data instances. For example, in an image classification problem, where a model wants to classify images, training data will include instances of known classes. A supervised Machine Learning algorithm analyzes the training data, learns from it, and then uses that learning to do various tasks. Supervised algorithms can also label unseen instances by mapping new instances with the learning knowledge from the training phase. However, it requires a generalization from the training data with the help of inductive bias. Supervised algorithms are widely used for various applications. The process of implementing a supervised algorithm to a problem domain is to adhere to the following steps.

A supervised algorithm strongly depends on training data. The performance is related to the choice of training data. It is crucial to choose the correct training data set. For example, in an animal image classification, the training data might be a single animal (cat, dog, etc.) or multiple animals of the same type. However, the training data set should be a representation of real-world instances. A set of input instances are gathered, and the corresponding output is also determined. This process can be done with the involvement of statistical measurements or human experts.

After selecting the training data, some featured need to be selected as a learning function. These features will be learned by the supervised algorithm and be used for future predictions. When a supervised algorithm learns better from the training dataset, it will show high accuracy. Generally, training data is transformed into a feature vector that represents the number of available features. The number of features should choose carefully since data can have multiple dimension and that makes complex for the algorithm to learn for better prediction.

The learning function structure needs to be predetermined since the availability of various supervised algorithms. Once these are done, the algorithm needs to be trained by the training data. Moreover, some supervised algorithm provides flexibility to choose certain parameter during the training phase. Those parameters are problem-specific and need to be adjusted adaptively. The performance evaluation of the supervised algorithm can be measured by a test set that is different from the training dataset. It will indicate the learning accuracy of the algorithm.

HOW SUPERVISED ALGORITHM WORKS

Supervised learning uses known data set to train an algorithm with features to make the prediction. This known data set is also known as training data which is associated with the desired output. Supervised learning establishes a model by learning the relationship between features and the output that which used afterward to make a prediction of a new dataset (test dataset).

Figure1. Supervised learning overview

Given a training set of an instance in the form of {(t1,f1)………(tn,fn)} where ti is the ith feature vector of training example, and fi is the label (known class). The supervised algorithm tries to map a function: T → F. this function is known as hypothesis space. Sometimes it is also used as a scoring function where the function returns a maximum score. There are various supervised learning algorithms that use a probabilistic model with the help of conditional probability, e.g., naïve Bayes. There are many supervised learning algorithms available now for various purposes. It is important to choose the right learning algorithm to use for the right problem domain. Choosing a supervised learning algorithm needs more considerations.

Bias-Variance Tradeoff

In supervised learning, bias indicates the difference between the average prediction of the model and the actual/ correct value of the desired prediction. If a supervised learning model has a high bias, that indicates that the model is very liberal to the training data and oversimplifies the model. If a model oversimplifies, then it will not learn complex patterns and associations of training data, and that can lead to high error on testing results. On the other hand, variance is an indicator of how much the estimate of target prediction will alter if training data are different. In other words, the amount of difference of random variable from the expected value. A model with high variance concentrates a lot on training data and does not generalized the data. But it also ends up doing a very high error rate in the testing result. There should be a trade-off between bias and variance to get a better prediction result. The prediction error is an indication of the balance of bias and variance. Moreover, the prediction error is the sum of bias and the variance (Allikmets, 1998).

Training Data Availability and Functional Complexity

Supervised learning depends on the quality of training data. The question is, how much data do a model needs to perform better? There is no exact answer to this problem. But it is well known that quality and amount of training data is the single most important factor for a model's performance. So, the answer for the amount of training data needed is –it depends. It depends on the task the model is trying to complete, the expected performance of the model, the available input features, the amount of noise in the training data and extracted features, and so on. For example, if the model is trying to predict something simple, then any algorithm with high bias and low variance will be able to learn from a small amount of training data. On the other hand, if the model is trying to achieve something complex which needs a lot of interactions among input features, it requires a large amount of training data. As mentioned above, there are no empirical bounds for required training data but based on experiments, and some suggestions were made by various researchers. In the case of regression, ten training instances for each class are required as per one in ten rule of thumb (Haldar, 2015). These numbers change for variations of the regression model. In computer vision, image classification using a deep neural network requires almost 1000 images per class (Warden, 2017). An efficient supervised learning model automatically adjusts the bias and variance trade-off with the amount of available training data.

The Dimensionality of Input Data

In various domains, the input dataset and the selected features may have multiple dimensions. The learning process for the model can be difficult in that case though the expected prediction may only depend on few features. The reason is, data with multiple dimensions confuse the algorithm, and as a result, the model has high variance. In this case, the model needs to be tuned in for low variance and high bias. Nonetheless, if irrelevant features can be removed manually or with the help of available feature selection algorithms, then the model can still perform better accurately.

Besides all those above-mentioned issues, some others factors can also influence choosing a learning algorithm. Heterogeneity of data plays a role. Heterogeneity means the feature vector includes various types – discrete, counts, continuous, etc. In that case, some algorithms are easier to implement and perform better. Supervised algorithms- linear regression, support vector machine, logistic regression, neural networks require numerical input features with a similar range. However, data redundancy is also important. If the input data is highly correlated, then there are numerical instabilities. Linear Regression, logistic regression will perform poorly for that type of data.

SOME SUPERVISED LEARNING ALGORITHM

Artificial Neural Network (ANN)

Nowadays, the most dominant supervised algorithms are based on Artificial Neural Network (ANN). The recent boom of various models makes many computer vision-related applications efficient. In the 1950s and 1960s, Frank Rosenblatt introduced the concepts in the name of “Perceptrons.” Perceptron takes several inputs and produces a single binary output. As figure 2 shows, it takes three inputs and produces one output. The number of inputs can be more or less.

Figure 2. Overview of the perceptron

The output is calculated based on the weights of the input. The weights of input indicate the importance of the input. The output 0 and 1 is determined whether the weighted sum (where wj is the weight and xj is the input itself) is greater or less than a threshold. In algebraic expression

In Machine Learning, ANNs are similar to the statistical model. All these models are inspired by the human brain structure. Artificial neural networks are a collection of neurons interconnected with each other that sends information to each other and produce the desired output. In computer vision, there are many neural networks used by various researchers. Some examples are Feedforward neural network, the simplest artificial neural network. The input travels in one direction and passes through input nodes towards the output. Sometimes it may consist of hidden layers, and it only uses front propagation. Feedforward Neural networks are very similar to perceptron's concept.

Radial basis function Neural Network takes account of the distance of any data points with respect to the center. It usually has two layers-one where features are combined and another the output layer. For distance, Euclidian measure is used mostly, but other measures are also used based on the problem domain. In addition,

Recurrent Neural Network (RNN) saves the output of a layer and feeds that back to the input to guide the prediction of that layer. The first layer is similar to a feedforward Neural network which calculated the product of the sum of all features and weights. The recurrent starts once those values are calculated, so for each step, the layers will remember some information. Moreover, in RNN, each layer works as a memory cell for computations. If the prediction is wrong, then some small changes can be made based on the learning rate, and that will improve the prediction result. RNN is widely used in the text-to-speech conversion.

Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) is a mostly used neural network for classification problems. It is very popular for groundbreaking accuracy. CNN is a deep learning algorithm that takes the input of an image and assigns weights and biases to various objects to the image. This capability makes different objects separable from each other. The architecture of CNN is related to the connectivity of neurons. It replicates the idea of the human brain to get prediction results. One important distinguishable characteristic of CNN is, it can capture the spatial and temporal dependencies in an image with filters. The basic CNN architecture is composed of convolution layer, pooling layer, and fully connected layer.

The convolution layer is the first part of the CNN. It consists of kernel/filters. The size of filters can be defined dynamically. These filters move from the beginning of an image to the end with a stride value. It traverses the whole image and performs a matrix multiplication, and puts all the results in a stack. All these results are combined with the bias, and that makes convoluted feature output or feature map. Each of the kernel creates one feature map. The size and depth of the filter depend on the input images. For example, an RGB image will have filters with a depth of three, and a binary image will have a depth of one. The main goal of the convolution layer is to extract features such as –edges, color, gradient orientation from the input image. The beginning convolutional layers extract basic features and as the network close to the end, the later cpnvolution layers learn specific features.

The pooling layer reduces the size of the convoluted features and reduces the dimension. The pooling layer extracts the dominant features, which is rational to the training data. There are two types of pooling layers- max pooling and average pooling. The max-pooling returns the maximum value of the part of the image convoluted by the kernel, and average pooling returns the average value. Nonetheless, a convolution layer and a pooling layer together represent a “layer” in CNN. Based on the complexity of the requirement, the number of layers can increase and decrease. However, the more layer will not guarantee the best predictions.

Each combination of convolution and pooling layers, generally followed by an activation function layer. There are many activation layers available now but the most researchers use Rectified Linear Unit (ReLU). The ReLU layers change any negative values of features map to zero. For this reason, all values passes to pooling layers are non-negative.

A fully connected layer (FC-Layer) is a feedforward neural network. The input of this layer is the output of the last pooling layer of the network. The output of the pooling layer is flattened (unrolling a three-dimensional matrix to a vector). The flattened vector is connected to fully connected layers and performs the same calculations. The final layer used a Softmax activation function (mostly ReLU) that provides the probabilities of any input value associated with the desired class.

Figure 3. Structure of a Convolutional Neural Network (CNN)
[@copyright of Mathworks]

Some prominent CNN architectures are known as- Alexnet, Googlenet, Resnet, VGG-16 and VGG 19. All these CNN is already trained with over a million dataset with a capability of classifying 1000 classes as mentioned in Imagenet. Those architectures are carefully designed and tested for the optimal performances. Researchers can use them for their own dataset. This method is known as “Transfer learning”. Transfer learning provides an easy way to classify images compared to build a CNN from scratch. Those prominent CNN models are open to modification for any specific classification task.

Naive Bayes Classifier

Naïve Bayes is a probabilistic classifier based on the Bayes theorem. It is a simple way of building a classifier that assigns a class label to the problem instances. It is highly scalable and requires a linear number of features for a problem. There are various names of naïve Bayes classifier- simple bias, independent bias. Regardless of their name, all naïve Bayes classifier assumes the value of a particular feature in separate from other values. In applications, naïve Bayes is a conditional probability model for classification.

(2)

All the features are represented by a vector, and that assigns a probability. But if the number of features is large, then building a model with this approach is inefficient. In addition to that, there is a Gaussian naïve Bayes which assumes that for continuous data, each class is distributed based on Gaussian distribution.

Support Vector Machines

Support vector machines (SVM) is a supervised Machine Learning algorithm for analyzing data and patterns. SVM constructs a set of a hyperplane in high dimensional spaces. These planes can be used for classification. It is a non-probabilistic binary classifier. For that reason, it can be applied to computer vision-related problems, especially classification. For example, human skin classification. During training, an annotated set of skin and non-skin pixels is given to the SVM for training. The SVM has a training algorithm that constructs a model that then attempts to assign pixels into the two training classes, thus making it a non-probabilistic binary linear classifier. An SVM model creates a representation of the pixels as points in space, mapped, so that skin and non-skin pixels are divided by clear and wide as a possible gap. New pixels are then mapped into the same space and predicted to fall in a category based on which side of the gap they fall on. (Han, 2006) utilized an SVM based on active learning to detect skin pixels for gesture recognition, claiming that it is transferable to other applications.

Discussion on Supervised Machine Learning Algorithms

Supervised machine learning algorithms are dominantly used to solve various computer vision problem nowadays. Artificial neural Network can detect complex nonlinear relationship between variables. It requires less statistical training and many of the algorithms are available. These algorithms can be used for classification and regression problems. However, in artificial neural network, user does not have the independence of making all the decisions, it works as a “black box”. The result of ANN highly depends on available good “training data”. In some domain of computer vision problem there are not many training data publicly available, e.g., medical image analysis. Nonetheless, K-nearest neighbors (KNN) is a simple algorithm and classify quickly compared to ANN. It can also handle noisy data. But, as the number of attribute increases, it becomes computationally expensive. As KNN gives equal importance to all attributes, the classification result is poor. Naïve bayes is a simple and useful algorithm for large dataset, it can be used for binary and multiclass classification. However, it is more complex and computationally expensive (Uddin, Khan, Hossain, & Moni, 2019). Overfitting for the model is also a problem for Naïve Bayes algorithm.

SUPERVISED LEARNING ALGORITHM IN COMPUTER VISION

Human Skin Detection

Computer vision has a wide variety of implementation of supervised learning. In this section, a well-known computer vision problem- human skin detection is discussed. Human skin detection is widely used primitive in image processing. Various applications over images with human content employ skin detection as a primary step. Skin detection is the process of discriminating human skin pixels from non-skin pixels in an image or video (Zuo, Fan, Blasch, & Ling, 2017). Various human-computer interaction (HCI) applications – face detection, face tracking (Mohanty & Raghunadh, 2016; Shakir1, George, & Tuama, 2015), gesture recognition (Rautaray & Agrawal, 2015), computational health informatics, web content filtering profoundly depends on efficient skin detection. Skin detection is also used as a primary step for detecting people in general. The main challenge of human skin detection is the large variations of human appearance with other objects. Besides, there are many factors that make skin detection a challenging problem. Among them, the notable one is variations in illumination. Variation in illumination is the most important factor that influences skin segmentation performance (St¨orring, Koˇcka, Andersen, & Granum, 2003). A variation of illumination (indoor, outdoor, shadows, presence of various light colors) produces changes in human skin tone. Shadows on the face from direct lighting may obscure some facial regions. It can be a reason for rendering a facial shape unevenly.

On the other hand, a bright facial region may be due to the natural diffusion of light, causing extra brightness on the face. Additionally, various ethnic groups have many skin tones. Skin color tone varies from person to person within the same ethnic group and also between members of different ethnic groups. Caucasians, Africans, and Asians have different skin hues ranging from white to dark to brown (Tan, Chan, Yogarajah, & Condell, 2011). Shadow in an image and complex background is another crucial part of correct skin detection. Many real-world background objects have similar skin colors. Skin color can be close to other image artifacts such as furniture, clothes, hair color, etc. The diversity is virtually unlimited. This causes skin detectors to produce false-positive detection (Kakumanu, Makrogiannis, & Bourbakis, 2007).In addition, image sources are an uncontrollable factor. Images available on the internet, newspapers may have been modified and filtered possibly multiple times. This makes color information problematic. Camera characteristics cause additional difficulty for skin detection. Because of these and other reasons, it is challenging to choose a specific trichromatic color space for any specific application. In addition, designing skin detection models to detect human skin under all possible constraints has not been achieved. For that reason, it is demanding to have a human skin detection method that can overcome the aforementioned challenges in separating skin and non-skin pixels.

Researchers have been working to find a technique that will be able to detect skin in spite of challenges. Skin detection can be considered a binary classification problem. There are two principal types of skin detection – pixel-based or region-based. In pixel-based detection, a pixel is classified compared with its neighbors as either a skin pixel or not. Skin detection that is based on specific trichromatic color space and specific color component value ranges is an example. On the other hand, region-based skin detection focuses on the spatial arrangement of skin pixels with additional information of intensity and texture. A few researchers have used texture (Dahmani & Larabi, 2014; Holden, Lee, & Owens, 2005; Kang, Lee, & Jung, 2004; Vadakkepat, Lim, De Silva, Jing, & Ling, 2008) information in combination with color-based methods. Wavelets, contourlets (Chai, Phung, & Bouzerdoum, 2001; Jaimes & Sebe, 2007) are among the textural features that have been used in conjunction with color cues. Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) have received wide attention more recently for image classification and object detection. But CNN's do not work well-defined relationship between a pixel and its neighbors. Since skin pixels are located close to each other spatially, CNN's lack the desired performance outcomes. Given all of the previous work, color cues have provided the dominant source of information. In spite of the emphasis on color-based analysis, a considerable number of applications will benefit from a system that can perform skin detection in the absence of color cues. Moreover, approaches mentioned above depend heavily on color information that, in the absence of global information, can lead to incorrect detection. Given the number of methods from which to choose, it is difficult to pick one which would project satisfactory accuracy under the diverse condition

Bayesian Statistics Based Skin Detection

In Statistical Models, skin detection is considered a probabilistic problem. There are many techniques based on the general distribution of skin and skin color that use specific color models in order to better detect skin. Based on an extensive training set, these strategies estimate the probability that a certain pixel is associated with the skin. These methods use Histogram, Lookup Tables, Distance-Based Detectors, Naive Bayes, and Gaussian Distribution.

Histogram-based (Non-parametric) To understand data, the plotting is a useful way. Besides, a histogram chart is another useful tool to represent data graphically, as shown in Figure 2. Each value that occurs in the data set has its own vertical bar. The shape of the histogram reveals the data's frequency distribution. The frequencies of the histogram are represented as a probability distribution. The height of a bin in the histogram is proportional to the probability (likelihood) that the color is a skin color with (equations 3 and 4). New pixels for which the corresponding likelihood value is greater than a predefined threshold θ are classified as skin pixels (equation 5), otherwise classified as background. (Gomez, 2002) proposed a simplified probability theory using RGB color space.

Figure 4. Histogram of an image

(3)

Where, P (rgb|skin) denotes the conditional probability of a pixel, whether it is a skin pixel.

(4)

Where P (rgb|∼ skin) denotes the conditional probability of a pixel whether it is a non-skin, a new pixel is labeled as skin if it satisfies a given threshold θ.

(5)

The main advantage of histograms is simple and easy to implement, which gives a good performance. However, histogram charts cannot give any spatial relationship between pixels, which makes it impossible to determine individual features of interest.

Lookup Tables (LUTs) is the fastest way for skin detection since it is not dependent on arithmetic operations. There is no specific definition of probability density characteristic in non-parametric techniques. Single histogram primarily based Look-UP-Table (LUT) model is a common approach in modeling skin color cluster. This technique uses a set of training skin pixels from the distribution of skin pixels in the color space chosen for the skin detection program. The training technique is simple but populating the histogram in this way calls for an extensive skin data set.

Bayes Classifier Approach is based on Bayes theorem. Bayesian classifier considers two histograms, skin and non-skin pixels, from training data normalized to provide probability distribution. The conditional probability density function is given using the Bayes rule (equation 6).

(6)

Where the conditional probability density function of a colored pixel, x, is P (skin|x). In addition, P (skin) and P (∼ skin) are the prior probabilities of skin and non-skin. P (x|skin) and p(x|∼ skin) are prior probabilities density of a given pixel x.

(Choi, Han, Chung, & Ryou, 2011) proposed a Bayesian classifier based on YCbCr color space. (Erdem, Ulukaya, Karaali, & Erdem, 2011) employed Bayesian-based post-filtering technique to reduce false positives of Viola-John face detector. (Zarit et al., 1999) performed a comparison study of the overall performance of the histogram method in Fleck HS, HSV, RGB, CIE Lab, and YCbCr. (Phung, Bouzerdoum, & Chai, 2005) examined the trade-off among the wide variety of bins in line with channel and detection rate.

There are numerous gains and drawbacks associated with histogram-based, also known as non-parametric techniques. The benefit of this method includes training and implementation, rapid training, and independence at the shape of the cluster (Vezhnevets, 2003). However, one major detriment of this method is their dependence on the training set that requires the gathering of a massive range of skin pixels. Furthermore, large memories are required to implement histogram-based models, especially when fine resolutions are required.

Parametric Models include pattern classification by Gaussian distribution. This method is a heuristic one and relies on skin color distribution that can be modeled based on Gaussian distribution in the color space. Most Gaussian distribution produces a symmetric, bell-shaped curve. The normal distribution depends on the mean µ and standard deviation σ. The mean determines the center, and the standard deviation determines height and width. The Probability Density Function (PDF) of a random variable x is given by P(x) (equation 7) (Duda, 2012).

(7)

Where, x is a normal random variable.

Single Gaussian method (SGMs), Gaussian mixture models (GMMs), the cluster of Gaussian models (CGMs), Elliptical models (EMs), etc., are evolved to compensate LUT shortcomings consisting of excessive storage requirement. Similarly, they generalize thoroughly with a tremendously smaller amount of training set. In SGM, there should be a smooth Gaussian distribution around the mean vector. GMM is advanced to version more complex distributions as a normalized weighted sum of Gaussian PDFs (Yang & Ahuja, 1998). It compensates the inability of SGM in managing out-of-control conditions in a general skin segmentation problem, similarly to the truth that SGM is not capable of approximating the actual distribution due to the asymmetry of distribution in its peak (Lee & Yoo, 2002). The most appealing features of GMM models are their easy evaluation procedure and occasional memory cost. The training technique is longer than the former methods. A comparative look at the overall performance of single and combination of Gaussian distributions in (Caetano, Olabarriaga, & Barone, 2003) confirmed that the mixture method improves the performance only in a relevant operating area. However, skin color clusters have an elliptical shape, and the use of the Gaussian model leads to a high false-positive rate. (Lee & Yoo, 2002) proposed an” elliptical boundary model” that outperforms SGM. The model is defined as (equation 8)

(8)

Non-parametric methods are primarily based on the histogram of skin and non-skin pixels in a predefined training set. In comparison to parametric models, histogram methods are independent of the shape of the cluster, trained efficiently. They can be utilized in several clock cycles per pixel needed for accessing memory. It was concluded that non-parametric models often outperform parametric ones with the cost of high storage requirements.

UNSUPERVISED MACHINE LEARNING

In Machine Learning, the problem of unsupervised learning is to ðnd hidden structure in unlabeled data. Unsupervised algorithms find the undiscovered pattern that is undiscoverable by the supervised algorithm and human observation. As there is no indication of possible output, the unsupervised algorithm cannot be trained. Moreover, unsupervised learning algorithms cannot directly apply for classification or regression since those algorithms do not know the output. Unlike the supervised algorithm that is bottlenecked by the requirement of quality training data, the unsupervised can find data pattern (Islam, 2019). In some real-world applications, where the quality of training data is sparse, the unsupervised algorithm can be used. Compared to supervised learning, unsupervised algorithms have some uncharted areas. There are many unsupervised algorithms available, but the most prominent are – clustering, principal component analysis, association rule, Apriori, etc.In this chapter, clustering algorithms will be discussed in details since it has the most widely used algorithm on computer vision.

CLUSTERING TECHNIQUE

Clustering is a division of data into groups of similar objects. In each group, named a cluster, there are objects that are compared and grouped based on identical and dissimilar properties. If data is represented in the cluster, it will lose some fine details but achieve simplification. Most data clustering problems are considered NP-hard. Clustering methods can be categorized into different paradigms: Partitional clustering, Hierarchical clustering, Density-based clustering, and Spectral clustering.

Partitional Clustering

As the name suggested, in partitional clustering, data is divided into non-overlapping subsets such that each data instance is assigned to exactly one subset. For example, K-means (Steinhaus, 1956) and K-medoids are the most famous example of this kind. K-means clustering applies an iterative approach. At first, it chooses the means of cluster commonly known as centroids. Afterward, it assigns data points to its nearest centroids. This approach is efficient in terms of computational speed and simple to use (Shapiro, 1992). However, the main shortcoming is the vulnerability of the random seeding technique. If the initial seeding points are not chosen carefully, the result will not be satisfactory. For this reason, an updated method name K-means++ (Arthur & Vassilvitskii, 2007) was proposed to improve. However, K-mediod is an improvement of K-means also to deal with discrete data, which takes the data point, most near the center of data points, as the representative of the corresponding cluster.

K-means Clustering, in this research, K-means clustering is used for clustering. K- means is a clustering method that divides data points into a predefined number of disjoint clusters. The” K” refers to the number of clusters that are defined prior to clustering. To decide data points belong to a specific cluster, various distance parameters are considered. Table 1 listed some distance parameter used for clustering

Table 2: Some distance parameter used for clustering

Distance Measure Equation Time Complexity
City Block Distance O(n)
Euclidean Distance O(n)
Average Distance O(n)
Czekanowski Coefficient O(2n)

K-means algorithm partitions are given data into a predefined number of clusters. The algorithm begins with a random set of K center points σ. For each iteration, data points are assigned to the nearest center points (equation 9). To decide the nearness, various distance measurements are used. Usually, only one assignment to the center is possible. If the distance between the center point is equal, then any one of the measurements is chosen at random. Initially, based on the number of clusters, random centroid values are assigned. It is shown in black in Figure 5.

Figure 5. K-means: Possible initial centroid positions

(9)

Afterward, the center points are repositioned according to the mean between the observation and respective center points (equation 10).

(10)

The update of the cluster will continue until all the observations remain unchanged to center points and when center points also remain unchanged. As stated before, the K-means algorithm attempts to optimize the objective function (equation 11). However, the number of observations and centroids are finite, so each iteration produces better clustering results. Figure 06 shows the initial cluster centroid positions after the first iteration. Figure 07 depicts the centroid positions after two-hundredth iterations.

Figure 6. K-means: First iteration
Figure 7. K-means: After two-hundredth iterations

(11)

with

The main disadvantage of K-means is the performance highly depends on initial cluster centroids. A wrong choice of centroids can possibly split data that belong together can cause some centroids to be more inclined to an outlier.

Hierarchical Clustering

In this approach, clusters are formed by either a bottom-up approach or a top-down approach. This dataset is represented by a dendrogram. At the initial level, clusters are formed along the hierarchy. Single-linkage clustering is a classic bottom-up approach in which data points are gradually agglomerated together to form clusters. For each step, all the pairwise distances are calculated to find a minimum. The points with minimum distance are linked together. Steps are repeated until all points are linked. This results in a tree structure, and the depth of the tree can be used from the cluster. Moreover, hierarchical clustering is suitable for the data set with different shapes and attributes of arbitrary type. However, the time complexity is relatively high, and several clusters are needed to be preset. Some mentionable algorithms for this category are BIRCH, CURE, and ROCK.

Density-Based Clustering

Density-based clustering is one of the well-known clustering techniques. This technique uses connectivity and density functions for clustering. In density-based clustering, data points are separated based on density, connectivity, and boundary. A cluster is formed at the base of the connected dense component and then is expanded to the direction of density. This property gives density-based clustering the ability to discover arbitrary shapes and remove the outliers. DBSCAN, OPTICS, DBCLASD, and DENCLUE are algorithms that are commonly used clustering techniques. The DBSCAN algorithm uses density-based notions to dene clusters. It utilizes two connectivity functions, density-reachable and density- connected to define each data point as either a core point or a border point. DBSCAN then visits points arbitrarily until all points have been visited. However, if the point is a core point, it attempts to expand and form a cluster around itself. Based on the experimental results, it tends to derive cluster robustness toward discovering arbitrarily shaped clusters.

Grid-Based Clustering

In grid-based clustering, the data points are divided into multiple portions (grids) at different granularity levels to be clustered individually. This method's processing time is significantly faster because it only concentrates on the statistical properties of certain grid portions. After calculating all the grids, the results are consolidated and analyzed. This approach makes it independent in performing clustering on the grid rather than the whole data set. This technique is highly grid-dependent, and the size of the grid plays a vital role in performances. For highly irregular data distribution, only some grids may not be formed adequately to achieve the correct result. A few well-known grid-based clustering methods are Wave Cluster, STING, and CLIQUE. In particular, CLIQUE is written so that it can automatically find subspaces with high-density clusters, and it does not require any assumption of data distribution. Its experimental results prove that this cluster can scale dimensions efficiently and, thus, it is exceptionally efficient in clustering high dimensional data.

Spectral Clustering

Various existing clustering methods use an iterative approach to achieve local minima and find functional clusters using different initial cluster starting points. In contrast, spectral clustering uses maximum eigenvectors that are provided from the distance matrix as a form of a matrix. Spectral clustering usually reduces the dimension of K-means clustering. It considers the range of the similarity matrix of the input data. These clustering methods treat the cluster as a graph partition. For that reason, it does not make any strong assumption for the statistical properties of a cluster; therefore, in terms of time complexity, it is very fast. Its drawbacks, however, are that it can be sensitive to the choice of parameters and computationally expensive for large datasets.

Discussion on Various Clustering Algorithms

Partitional algorithms apply mainly to numerical datasets. However, a variation of K-means named K-mode has been developed for categorical data. Moreover, they are based on a certain assumption to partition a dataset. Thus, they need to specify the number of clusters prior. The results of clustering are devoted to similar points that belong to the same cluster. Those points can be centroid or mediods, which depend on the clustering algorithm used. However, regardless of algorithm choice, the object remains the same- Minimization of distance within the same cluster and maximization of distance among separate clusters. Also, a centroid of a K-means represents the minimum distance of data points that belongs to the same cluster. Hierarchical clustering methods create a hierarchical decomposition of the database represented as a dendrogram. This algorithm is a more effective method for detecting outlier and handling noisy data. BIRCH uses a hierarchical data structure called CF-tree for multi-phase clustering. In BIRCH, the first scan determines a good clustering and more scan improves the cluster quality. But it is only applicable for numerical data. Besides, it is order sensitive, which means, for the same data points, it can demonstrate multiple clusters but in a different order. However, it has performance limitations for un-uniform data size and shape. The reason is, it heavily depends on the centroid value when data points are redistributed at the final stage. So, cluster quality is parameter depended. Density-based clustering algorithms suitably handle arbitrarily shaped collections of points as well as clusters of different sizes. Moreover, they can efficiently separate noise. Grid-based algorithms perform well for large databases. It can also find various arbitrary shapes of the cluster with the detection of outliers. A well-known grid-based algorithm is STING

Cluster Validity Assessment

Clustering results need to be evaluated, and the process is not straightforward. To evaluate the cluster, it is required to consider the partition, which represents best the purpose of the aim. This evaluation process is known as cluster validity. In general terms, there are three approaches to investigate cluster validity. The first is based on external criteria. This implies that the results of a clustering algorithm are evaluated based on a pre-specified structure. The second approach is based on internal criteria. In this approach, the cluster is assessed based on various proximity matrices. The last one uses a relative measurement. Also, cluster evaluation is done with comparisons of another cluster. This cluster is a result of the same cluster but at that time with different parameters. There are two criteria proposed for clustering evaluation and selection of an optimal clustering scheme (Berry & Linoff, 2004). One of them is compactness. This property depicts how close two clusters are to each other. A standard measure of compactness is the variance, which should be minimized. The other one is Separation. It measures the difference between the two clusters. Some common approaches are measuring the distance between two different clusters:

Single linkage measures the distance between the closest members of the clusters (equation 12).

(12)

Where G denotes the set G = {i1, i2......it} of indices points of the group and H is the dissimilarity score. However, single linkage suffers from a chaining problem. In other words, if two groups need to be merged, it needs one pair of close points avoiding others. As a result, the cluster can be very spread and not well compact. Complete linkage, measures the distance between the most distant members

(13)

Complete linkage avoids the aforementioned chaining problem, but it suffers from crowding. In addition, its score H is based on worst case dissimilarity among pairs. So, the result can be compact, not separate distinguished enough. Comparison of centroids measures the distance between the centers of the clusters. These two approaches are based on statistical tests, and their major drawback is their high computational cost. On the other hand, average linkage aims at finding the best clustering scheme that a clustering algorithm can be defined with certain assumptions and parameters.

Validity Indices

Quantitative evaluation of clustering results is known as cluster validity methods. There are three criteria-external, internal, and relative criteria. In an external criteria approach, data points are examined to conclude whether or not they are randomly structured. This analysis is based on the Null Hypothesis expressed as a statement of the random structure of a dataset. This hypothesis is based on statistical tests, which lead to a computationally complex procedure. For internal criteria, the aim is to evaluate the clustering result of an algorithm using only quantities and features inherent to the dataset. Statistical testing is the basis of the above-mentioned validation methods. Thus, the main setback is, since it needs consideration for internal or external criteria, it needs a large computation. For this, a different validation approach-relative criteria are proposed. It is based on relevant criteria, excluding the requirement of any statistical tests. Ultimately it chooses the best clustering approach for the prior defied scheme based on the criterion specified. However, the clustering method that is adopted in this study K-means clustering. It is an example of crisp clustering since the clustering object may have a membership of either skin or non-skin category. To evaluate the crisp clustering, the well-known Dunn and Dunn-like indices are used. A cluster validity index for crisp clustering was proposed in (Dunn,1974), which attempts to identify compact and well-separated clusters and gives the number of clusters. The index is defined as (equations 14, 15, 16) for a specific number of clusters.

UNSUPERVISED LEARNING ALGORITHM IN COMPUTER VISION

Color Based Image Segmentation

In computer vision, a significant real world problem is image segmentation. Segmentation of an image means to divide an image into various regions based on the pixel characteristics. These regions can be used to identify objects or boundaries which can be used for further analysis. In addition, segmentation of an image allows us to separate foreground from a background, identify the location of an object in the image and also locate the boundary that separates two objects. In practice, cameras can capture a lot of objects within one image. The separation/ segmentation is crucial for current real-world problem solving.

Segmentation techniques separates or distinguish objects based on the pixel’s information. A typical image can contain millions of individual pixels and segmentation allows us to separate pixels with similar characteristics from others. For this reason, clustering is a good choice for image segmentation. In medical image processing segmentation is particularly useful, where the doctor wants to separate healthy cells from the cancerous cells. Clustering techniques, for example K-mean clustering will separate objects with similar properties (shape, color, etc.,) other from object different from them.

A simple color based segmentation of image example is given below

Figure 8. Clustering based image segmentation example. (Image source: Wikipedia)

In this example, a K-means clustering is used to segment the images based on color information. As the figure shows, the original butterfly images are segmented to three different cluster. One of them contains the butterfly, others the different flowers present in the image.

CONCLUSION

In this chapter, some various types of Machine Learning algorithms are described focusing on Computer vision. The research area of Computer vision is large, and also its implementations. Emerging Machine Learning certainly helps the pace of advancement of computer vision. New algorithms are invented by various researchers, and they have their own place in the learning paradigm. However, a lot of students are interested in learning those novel Machine Learning techniques and their possible implementations. This chapter can be a starting point for those interested readers.

Vadakkepat, P., Lim, P., De Silva, L. C., Jing, L., & Ling, L. L. (2008). Multimodal approach to human-face detection and tracking. IEEE Transactions on Industrial Electronics , 55(3), 1385–1393. doi:10.1109/TIE.2007.903993

Vezhnevets, V., Sazonov, V., & Andreeva, A. (2003, September). A survey on pixel-based skin color detection techniques . Proc Graphicon, 3, 85–92.

Yang, M.-H., & Ahuja, N. (1998). Gaussian mixture model for human skin color and its applications in image and video databases. In Storage and retrieval for image and video databases vii (Vol. 3656, pp. 458–467). doi:10.1117/12.333865

KEY TERMS AND DEFINITIONS

Computer Vision: A research area of computer science that deals with image and video. The goal is to extract important information extraction from images and video.

Human Skin Detection: Process to detect human skin from images or videos. It has various applications in computer vision.

Machine Learning: A part of the artificial intelligence domain. It consists of various algorithms which can be used to analyze data and get some insights from the data.

Supervised Learning: A technique of a Machine Learning algorithm. It uses known data to get some prediction about unknown data with the statistical model.

Testing Data: It is a dataset without known labels/ information. Computer models used that data for real-life applications.

Training Data: It is a dataset with a known label. This type of data is used to train a computer model for the desired application.

Unsupervised Learning: A Machine learning technique. It does not need any known data. It discovers various data information by itself.

Chapter 3

  • Ladly Patel
    Sri Krishna Institute of Technology, India

ABSTRACT

Humans communicate with each other and express emotions using facial expressions. Facial expression is an important part of expressing emotions. Facial features can be considered as eyes, mouth, and nose. In this chapter, the authors considered these facial features for emotion detection and processed them with convolutional neural network (CNN). There are mainly six basic types of emotions: fear, disgust, anger, sadness, happiness, and surprise. These emotions can be classified into two types: positive emotions and negative emotions. A positive emotion is a feeling where there is no negativity such as happy, neutral. A negative emotion is a feeling of depression, frustration including anger, sadness, fear. This chapter describes a step-by-step method in processing an image in CNN and giving an output. CNN classifies different emotions. Further classification is done for emotion as negative and positive, and when the negative emotion is detected, music is played to change the emotion of a person.

INTRODUCTION

A convolutional neural network (CNN) is a neural network consisting of one or more number of convolutional layers which is used in image processing, classification of objects or images, segmentation. It is mainly used to process large set of data and only one CNN algorithm can extract features, classify an object or image and give a desired fast. CNN processing is much faster. CNN consists of three layers and they are first is convolutional layer, second is pooling layer and last is fully connected layer. CNN has many applications but mostly it is used in image related application. Some application of CNN are image classification, image segmentation, object detection in images, weather forecasting, etc. The research work contains information about image processing and classification of to detect the emotions by analysing facial features, algorithm and its code snippets to process an image and classify it to get the desired output. It is also shown that how an image is getting processed step by step in different layers of convolutional layer with output. For all the layers of CNN, various equations are shown in this chapter. In this chapter, CNN is detecting emotions of person and then classify emotion into positive and negative emotion and in later stage negative emotions are considered and musical therapy is given so that person mood is changed from negative to positive emotions. Here only two positive emotions is considered and they are happy and neutral, whereas only two negative emotions are considered for this chapter and they are sad and angry. Deformation in facial expressions from negative to positive emotions are been extracted and plotted. Here through sequence diagram the overall flow in the architecture is shown. In this chapter, dataset of different emotions are also shown and sample dataset also shown. For all the emotions, histogram is plotted. CNN confusion matrix was also shown here and in a table manual prediction and neural network prediction is shown.

BACKGROUND

The term Convolutional neural networks was first introduced in the year 1980 by Yann LeCun. Convolutional neural network (CNN) is a class of deep learning neural network. Convolutional networks was inspired by observing the patterns between neurons in animal visual cortex. A convolutional neural network is a kind of artificial neural network which is used in image recognition and then processing which is designed to process a pixel data. CNN consists of an input layer, hidden layer and an output layer. CNN extract features from the images. The input layer in CNN is a grayscale image. The output layer consists of multi-class labels. Hidden layer consists of convolution layer, ReLu layer, Pooling layer and a fully connected neural network. To train CNN, each input image is need to pass through multiple series of convolution layers with kernals, ReLu layer, Pooling layers and at last to fully connected layer so that an object or image can be classified with probability values ranging between 0 and 1. For this chapter, some survey were done to proof that CNN is best for processing and classifying a images. Some of the surveys are listed below:

  • • In a paper, published in year 2012 by Deng J, the author describe about an hierarchical framework for detecting emotion and it consist of two layers, the first one is external layer that show preliminary and superficial emotions and the second one is inherent layer that shows psychic and resonant emotions. A music emotion model called Resonance Arousal Valence model is built depending on this two given layer. This model display emotions in a three dimensional and continuous space. Based on features like intensity, pitch, harmony, etc., music will be passing to the given model. To efficiently illustrate music with many emotion labels a supervised multiclass label is implemented using KNN method.
  • • In a paper, published in 2015 by H.Jing, author had designed cognitive emotional model inside a robot for monitoring elder people. By combining Gabor filter, k-Nearest neighbour algorithm and Local Binary Pattern algorithm facial features is extracted and recognized, and. Facial features have effect on robot emotional state and is called as AVS emotion space. After this optimization of smart home environment on the cognitive emotional model is analysed using simulated annealing algorithm. Then, lastly transformation probability from different emotional state is obtained using cognitive reappraisal strategy and Euclidean distance. The simulation and experiment have been tested to reduce negative emotions.
  • • In a paper, published in 2019 by Jain, the author describe about classifying image into different emotions using Deep Convolutional Neural Networks(DNNs).The database which is used to train images are Cohn-Kanade and JAFE dataset.
  • • In the paper, published in 2015 by Kuo, the author describe about emotion based music system where a fuzzy logic controller is used to control the rate of music and an Adaptive Partition genetic algorithm to make corresponding melodies. The chords are produced with the help of music theory and the instrumentation is done towards the concept of probability. Here all the steps can be given by Virtual Studio Technology to a user who wants to listen songs for different emotions.
  • • In a paper, published in 2014 by Lozano, the author describe about identifying emotions of an elderly person using webcam. For identifying face features, Active shape model is used. For training JAFFE and MMI databases are used and Support Vector machine is used for classifying the facial expressions of an elder person.
  • • In a paper, published in 2015 by Perikos, the author describe about an emotion identification system where it identify six emotion which is proposed by Ekman for face. Using Viola-Jones algorithm face is detected in images. After this it locates and calculates the characteristics for a particular region and then extract features and geometrical view from each area. For classification of this features based on facial expressions, a Support Vector Machine classifier and a Multilayer Perceptron Neural Network is used which identify particular emotions. JAFFE and Kohn Kanade databases are used for doing the experiment.
  • • In a paper, Published in 2012 by Piatkowska, the author describe about detection of spontaneous emotions using a recognition system which is implemented by support vector machine system as a classifier. Here a face representation is created using a face detection algorithm called viola -jones and the face texture is then encoded with Local Binary Patterns and the feature are used to identify emotion.
  • • In a paper, Published in 2017 by Sarkar R, the author define low level time domain and extracted spectral features from music. Time and frequency are the features needed to match a user view about any emotion. For classification, unsupervised method is followed. The two clustering algorithm which are used are K-means and Agglomerative clustering. Experiment is done on a standard dataset by combining features and clustering algorithms.
  • • In a paper, Published in 2012 by Sourina, the author defined about EEG enabled music therapy algorithm which is used to identify the user’s emotions and based on the emotion as input, the music therapy session will be adjusted to treat a user without the help of music therapist. The given emotion recognition algorithm can recognize six emotions like fear, frustrated, sad, happy, pleasant and satisfied. This algorithm is based on Arousal-Valence emotion model and it can identify all emotions that can be described by the 2-dimensional model. It uses International Affective Digitized Sounds (IADS) database to get sound for different emotions.
  • • In a paper, published in 2013 by Y.Li, the author described a framework called Dynamic Bayesian Network for tracking facial features and recognizing the expression of face.

In all the above approaches, the limitations we identified as over fitting and tilted faces cannot be detected using Viola-Jones. The EEG enabled algorithm works only with 2D model. For KNN method, initially need to determine value of nearest neighbours and the computation cost is high. In K-means algorithm it does not work properly with global cluster and there is no backtracking in Agglomerative clustering algorithm. In support vector machines (SVM), training and testing data size should be small and take more time to train the data.

So, it is seen that how CNN is efficient and faster to process and image. There are some applications of CNN which are listed below:

  • • CNN is widely used in image recognition and object detection.
  • • CNN can be used widely in understanding the climate over a geographic area.
  • • It play great role in advertisement world.
  • • It is used in facial recognition applications

MAIN FOCUS OF THE CHAPTER

In this chapter, it is shown how to process an image using Convolutional neural network (CNN) across different layers of CNN and features are extracted and classified. Human express their emotions mostly through the face and emotions affect the behavior and mood of a person. If the mood a person is not good due many reasons like work stress etc. so they will mostly listen songs to refresh themselves or to boost their mood. It can also be used to cure mood swings without manual interference. Manually selecting the songs from a playlist is time consuming task. So to overcome this, an automatic emotion based music played using deep learning approach called convolutional neural network is shown in this chapter which will monitor the face of a person using camera and detect the emotions and based on that emotions plays a music to make user mood good. In this chapter, considering two negative emotions sad and anger to detect by deep learning and play music automatically according to it. It is shown with histogram of emotions and by playing music negative emotions transformed to positive emotions such as happy and neutral. When the person mood detected as happy or neutral, the music will automatically stop.

So, the main aim of the chapter is to describe about detecting negative emotions such as anger and sad by analysing facial expression with the help of deep learning approach called Convolutional Neural Networks. Music will be played to change it to positive emotions such as happy and neutral. Once the positive emotions detected, music will be automatically stop. It will also plot transformation graphs of positive and negative emotions before and after playing music. It will also plot error rate and accuracy rate graphically.

This chapter concepts can be used as psychotherapy to change from negative emotions such as sad and anger to positive emotions such as happy, neutral by automatically playing music. This system can be used in companies, home, school, colleges and hospitals as psychotherapy to treat anger and sadness. The given system can also be used in hospital for pain management in cancer patients by giving music therapy. It can also be used in car to monitor the driver emotions and change it if it is found to be negative.

Face is considered as most prominent part of human beings. Various emotions can be expressed using eyes, mouth etc. like sad, happy, anger etc. According to “Discovering Psychology” book by Don Hockenbury and Sandra E. Hockenbury, an emotion is a compound psychological state which consists of three unique components: a subjective experience, a physiological response, and a behavioral response. So, in general emotions are mostly called as feelings which have experiences such as anger, hate, joy, love etc. According to psychologist Paul Eckman, 1972 defined that mostly six emotions and they are universally present in the entire human society and they are disgust, fear, surprise, sadness, anger and happiness. Emotions are classified as positive and negative emotion which is linked with a specific pattern of physiological activity. Many researches have been done in past decades on emotion considering different areas like psychology, neuroscience, medicine, etc. Positive emotions are feeling where there is no pain or discomfort. Frederickson in year 2009 recognizes the ten most frequent positive emotions such as gratitude, interest, joy hope, pride, love, inspiration, awe, serenity and amusement. There are several health benefits if a person is filled with positive emotions like blood pressure will be normal, reduced the chances of heart diseases, healthy weight, normal level of blood sugar, long life and many more. When positive thoughts are present in people they feel happy, cortisol decreases, and the brain produces serotonin and this create a feeling of well-being.

Negative emotion is described as feeling that causes a person to feel dejected or unhappy. The most common negative emotions are annoyance, sadness anger, guilt, anxiety, fear, despair, discouragement, apathy, frustration and disappointment. The disadvantage of having negative emotions on health like creating chronic stress and which leads to change in level of hormone, exhaust the brain chemicals needed for happiness and harms the immune system. If negative emotions persist for longer time in human beings that can lead to trigger a brain part area called as amygdala, that is responsible for fear and anxiety. If a person is angry or in a stress for longer time then heart rate increases due to release of adrenaline hormone, testosterone production increases. Cortisol increases and which lead to high blood pressure, change in mood, muscle weakens, change in breathing pattern, etc. Various emotions like sad, happy, anger, etc. can be expressed using eyes, mouth, etc. By analysing the facial expression of a person, we can tell the current mood of a person. According to psychology, a mood is described as an emotional state whereas emotions, moods are less particular and intense and mostly invoked by an event. A person while communicating with another person uses facial expression to express his or her emotions. Analysing facial expression to detect emotion of person with the help of computer is challenging task due to the background elements, lighting conditions, pose and facial expressions. Detecting emotions using facial expression automatically via computer is currently in trend. This can be used in different applications like monitoring the behaviour of students inside a classroom, driver mood analysis, old age health monitoring, boosting up mood of a person if he or she is feeling low, and can also be used in psychotherapy session, etc. According to a survey worldwide, more than 154,000 people in 147 countries throughout 2017 were sad, worry, angry or in pain. Data given by World health organization that 300 million people all over world suffer from depression and from research the result came that India is the most depressed country in the world. There are many ways by which a person can change the mood if he or she is feeling low like doing workout, watching inspirational movies or videos, meeting family and friends, listening to music, etc. Here we will be discussing how listening to music can help in changing the negative emotion of person to positive emotion.

Music plays a very important role in a person's daily life. Music can be used for entertainment or for boosting the mood of a person. Presence of music in human’s life started when first civilization of humans started on earth long back ago. So, from that time music became an integral part in society and culture. Initially music consists of simple tunes but as time passed, different variety and complexity of music increases like having different types, rhythms, keys, etc. There are different kind of music like fast tempo and slower-paced music. Listening to fast tempo music for longer period can cause several health problems like damaging ear drums, increase in cortisol and noradrenalin production and this two hormones are produced in body when a fighting situations occur. Prolong exposure to fast tempo may lead to several health problems like depression, frustration. Whereas listening to slower paced music, soft music or classical music soothes brain and relax the person if there is stress and boost the mood of person. Listening to soft music will relax body and mind and people can use it to refresh the mood. Soft music change the person mood from negative emotion like sad or angry to positive mood like happy. The benefits of listening classical music leads to increase the blood flow to different brain areas, activating different cognitive and emotional centres. Listening to soft music release the hormone called dopamine. Dopamine is a chemical which transmit signals between nerve cells of brain and also act as mood booster. According to studies listening to classical music increase learning capabilities of a person, lowered blood pressure, helps in managing pain in cancer patients and stress reduction. At Duke Cancer Institute, researchers discovered that there was reduction in pain and anxiety after biopsy while listening to classical music. A 2013 research published in the Journal of Positive Psychology discover that person listening upbeat music can enhances the mood and increase the happiness level within two weeks. Managing stress, increasing memory, and reducing pain using music therapy session suggested by the American Music Therapy Association (AMTA). So, it depends on what type of music a person chooses to play for changing the mood like soft music to reduce the stress.

For people having Alzheimer’s disease and dementia, listening to music provide support in emotional and behavioural way suggested by researcher. Alzheimer’s is a condition where memory is destroyed slowly slowly and other crucial mental functions. In Alzheimer’s disease, musical memories are preserved in brain areas that are linked with musical memory and remain unharmed by the disease. So, music is used as psychotherapy for people suffering from Alzheimer’s disease and dementia. While listening to music, any person can train an Alzheimer patient with the music and this musical memory will be preserved in brain.

Patients can also communicate their feelings while listening to music. Listening to music helps the release of sleep hormone called Melatonin which leads to better sleep if a person is suffering from insomnia. Listening to soft and happy music can decrease the level of negative emotions like sad or anger in a human being. A person should not have these negative emotions for a long time as it leads to affecting the body and the mind of a person by disturbing their sleeping pattern, increasing blood pressure, hormonal imbalance, etc. If a person is sad for a longer period of time, then slowly the intensity of negative emotion increases and lead to depression if left untreated. Depression is called as a mood disorder followed by always having a tendency of feeling sad and lack of interest to do any work in day-to-day life. Music therapy can be used to cure depression, sadness, and anger, which consists of vocal and instrument form of music to cure patients.

Music therapy stimulates different parts of the brain. Music can positively affect regions of brain that control anxiety and stress and lead to the release of various hormones such as dopamine which makes a person feel good, prolactin is a hormone that helps to bond people together and oxytocin is a hormone which helps in creating a feeling of trust. Listening to motivational songs during a music therapy can help to cure depression. At each side of brain there is a temporal lobe present just above the ears called auditory cortex. This area’s brain cells are arranged systematically by sound frequency and are sensitive to high and low frequency. The auditory cortex analyzes the music and collects information on volume, pitch, speed, and melody. Thus, music can affect our mood due to the presence of rhythm and tone that a person hears while listening to music. While listening to music, our heart synchronizes with it. Music pitch, rhythm, and meter are processed in both left and right hemispheres of the brain, and classical music and soft music can be used as psychotherapy for patients.

So, here a system is described using deep learning approach which will automatically detect emotions of person using facial expression in a live video. In this chapter, it will be shown how with the help of music there will be transformation from negative emotion to positive emotions. For this only two negative emotion is considered and such as anger and sad and it will be transformed to positive emotion such as happy and neutral. In live video face of person is sent frame by frame as input is sent as raw image for pre-processing. After this step, pre-processed image is fed as an input to convolutional neural network where features are extracted and classified as negative or positive and emotion can be identified. If the emotion detected as negative emotion such as sad then automatically a happy music will be played and continuously through camera person facial expression is monitored and processed and when it finds that person emotion is changed to positive emotion such as happy, then music will be automatically stops. This type of application can be used as psychotherapy to cure sadness, anxiety and depression, Alzheimer’s disease and dementia and pain management.
