Seda Khadimally has extensive academic research, writing, editing, and publishing experience and expertise gained over the course of two decades. She published her first edited book entitled, “Technology-Assisted ESL Acquisition and Development for Nontraditional Learners” with IGI Global. As a prolific author, she has presented her published papers in regional, national, and international conferences in prestigious academic institutions. Having written numerous articles, book chapters, comprehensive book reviews, as well as other high-quality scholarly works in well-acclaimed academic journals, she currently serves as a peer reviewer and a member of various Editorial Advisory Boards of well-established scholarly journals in the fields of Educational Leadership and Educational Technology. Following her Bachelor of Arts (BA) degree earned in English Language and Literature (English Philology) at Istanbul University, she studied International Business Administration at the University of California at Berkeley, where she was awarded with distinction. Next, Dr. Khadimally received her first master's degree in Education (M.Ed.) in Educational Management and Control at Yeditepe University. She then earned a Master of Liberal Arts (ALM) degree in the field of Educational Technologies at Harvard University. She was finally awarded with a Doctorate degree in Education (Ed.D.) in the field of Educational Leadership with a Specialization in Educational Technology by the School of Advanced Studies (SAS) at the University of Phoenix (UOPX). The topic of her doctoral dissertation is “A Phenomenological Exploration of Adult ESL Students Utilizing Mobile Learning Technologies.” In addition to her academic affiliation with North American University (NAU) as a faculty member since 2020, Dr. Khadimally has consecutively served in several higher educational organizations including Harvard University, Rice University, and Stanford University since 2007. Also, she currently teaches and leads as an educational leader both onsite and online and in all grade levels in various other educational organizations in Houston, particularly in the areas of English language teaching, master’s and doctoral level scholarly research and writing processes, advanced academic writing, reviewing and editing, instructional coaching, academic and peer mentorship, training of professional learning communities, and more.

Ibrahim Eren Bisen is a researcher at RUSMP at Rice University.

Naciye Celebi received the B.S. Degree in Computer Science from North American University in 2017, the M.S. degree in Digital Forensics from Sam Houston State University in 2019. She is currently a Ph.D. student in the Department of Computer Science - Digital and Cyber Forensics Program at Sam Houston State University. She has been a researcher at the Cyber Forensics Intelligence Center. Her current research is concerned with Multimedia Forensics. She has worked on Machine Learning and Deep Learning applications on research projects.

Mustafa Duran is currently an instructor at North American University, where he teaches Introduction to Computer Science, Systems Programming, Operating Systems, and Data Structures courses. Also, he has so far taught core Java for the QA Automation Engineering Bootcamp for 3 terms. Professor Duran earned his Bachelor’s degree in Computer Education from Marmara University in Istanbul, Turkey, in 1999. After college, he continued his career as an instructor at Fatih University in Istanbul, Turkey. He then earned his Master’s degree in Computer Science from the same institution in 2011. Duran’s Master’s thesis was published in the format of an academic research book by the Lambert Publishing company. Following his master’s studies, Professor Duran started his PhD studies at Yildiz Technical University in Istanbul, Turkey, in 2012. During this time, he successfully completed the following courses: Image Processing, Artificial Intelligence and Machine Learning, in addition to Iris Segmentation, CNN News Classifications, Video Tracking, and more. He moved to the U.S.A. in 2018, and is married with two amazing children. Professor Duran has lived in Richmond, TX, since 2019. Among his hobbies are cycling, seeing new places, and travelling. He is also highly interested in mechanics and fixing things in his daily life. Finally, he very much enjoys spending his leisure time with his family, especially on holidays and by going out on family vacations.

Dennis Foung is a lecturer in Arts Studies in Research and Writing (ASRW) in the School of Journalism, Writing, and Media at The University of British Columbia (UBC). He holds a doctorate in language education and a number of academic qualifications in the fields of language studies, human resource management and development, professional and vocational education, and data science. Prior to joining ASRW, Dennis was a member of the Learning Analytics team at the UBC’s Centre for Teaching, Learning, and Technology. He also taught academic literacy for more than 10 years in Hong Kong and Canada. Dr. Foung has a keen interest in big data research and its application in the teaching and learning context. He has published papers in journals and book chapters, as well as served as a principal investigator in various analytics-based projects.

Nurefsan Guneri is a researcher at RUSMP at Rice University.

Tze-Li Hsu received his doctoral degree in Sociology at Florida State University in 2014, and he is a tenure track faculty in the department of Sociology at Sam Houston State University since 2015. As an assistant profession of Sociology, he teaches various courses at the undergraduate/graduate level, including Social Statistics, Introduction to Ethnic Studies, and Sociology of Aging. He is interested in the intersection of mental health, aging, gender difference, and marital status in aging and the life course perspective. He has advanced statistical training including structural equation modeling, latent growth curve analysis and managed multiple large-scale projects throughout his time in graduate school. His scholarly work can be seen in Sociological Spectrum and Asian Population Studies. His research examines the association between marital status and mental health, with special attention to variations across age groups and to dimensions of psychological well-being among the elderly, using data from the Midlife and Development in the United States (MIDUS). Also, he is interested in the potential mediating factors in the socioeconomic status and well-being association data from the Health and Retirement Study (HRS). His current agenda focuses on this topic, drawing on interdisciplinary theories, advanced statistical methods, and a focus on age and the broader dimensions of mental health and well-being for the elderly. His research on these topics has led to several papers published, under review or revision and has won Sociological Spectrum 2013 Outstanding Paper Award. He has frequently presented his research findings at national conferences, meetings, and workshops.

A.B.M. Rezbaul Islam is an Assistant Professor at the Department of Computer Science in the Sam Houston State University, Texas, USA. He received his Ph.D. in Computer Science and Computer Engineering from the University of North Texas, USA. During his Ph.D., he works with medical image processing in a collaboration with the University of Iowa and Mayo Clinic, Minnesota. He has published multiple journals and conference papers on image processing, Machine learning, and data mining. Before his Ph.D., he was working in the Software industry for multiple years. He received BSc in Computer Science and Engineering from Shah Jalal University of Science and Technology, Bangladesh. MSc degree in Computer Engineering from Ajou University, South Korea. His current research interests include Image Processing, Computer Vision, and Machine Learning.

Joanna Kwan is currently a project associate who works on university-wide initiatives in Communities of Practice and Teaching Excellence at The Hong Kong Polytechnic University. She participated in various government-funded research projects on English for Academic Purposes, Writing Across Disciplines, English Across the Curriculum, Hong Kong English, Language (Education) Policies and Learning Analytics in Hong Kong universities. She studied English Language and Linguistics at the University of York, UK, and Applied Linguistics at The University of Edinburgh, UK. She holds a CELTA qualification. https://orcid.org/0000-0001-5569-9179.

Qingzhong Liu is currently an Associate Professor at Sam Houston State University. His research interest includes digital forensics, cybersecurity, multimedia computing and computer vision, bioinformatics, computational intelligence, and computing applications. He was the recipient of the Fraud Impact Award at Great Houston in 2015 and the SHSU Excellence in Scholarly and Creative Activity Award in 2017. He has been serving on the Technical Program Committee of the ACM Workshop on Information Hiding and Multimedia Security since 2013, and on the Technical Program Committee and one of area chairs for the ACM Multimedia Conference since 2019. In 2020, Stanford University ranked him among the world’s top 2% scientists in Artificial Intelligence and Image Processing. As a sole inventor, he has three patents in multimedia forensics. His study has been funded by the US Army, NSF, NIJ, and NIH.

Trinh Ngoc Thanh holds a PhD Degree in English from Tamkang University, Taiwan. He is currently a lecturer of English at the Faculty of Foreign Languages at Ho Chi Minh City University of Technology and Education. His major research interests include Corpus Linguistics, L2 Writing, and International Relations. His recent publication is featured in the book titled Technology-Assisted ESL Acquisition and Development for Nontraditional Learners (published by IGI Global).

Yusuf Ozgel is a researcher at RUSMP at Rice University.

Ladly Patel is an Assistant Professor in the Department of Computer Science and Engineering, in Sri Krishna Institute of Technology. She has completed her Mtech in Big data from Dayananda Sagar University. She has published 6 papers in reputed journals and also attended 3 conferences.

Mahbubur Rahman is an Assistant Professor in the department of Computer Science at North American University, and has been working here since January 2020. He completed his Master’s in Computer Science from Texas Tech University in December 2010, and earned a PhD degree in Computer Science from the University of Houston in May 2015. He worked as a postdoctoral fellow at the University of Texas Medical Branch for two years after receiving his PhD degree. He then worked as a software engineer in California for two years. Dr. Rahman has extensive research and development experience in the fields of machine learning, data mining, and algorithm optimization both within the industry and academia. During his industry research career, he developed a novel approach to extracting the distinguishable features from the unstructured data sets and did a multidimensional analysis on the structured data sets in order to find out interesting groups. Dr. Rahman has significantly contributed to academic research, as well. He introduced a data modeling-enabled machine learning algorithm that can enhance the predictability of the algorithms. His cluster-based prediction model developed at the postdoctoral research level is being used in different research projects. He is applying these deep learning models to optimize the algorithms, along with their conversions, to different programming environments. Dr. Rahman has published five journals and two conference papers so far in his field of research. He is very much interested in exploring the applications of machine learning and artificial intelligence in Education.

Faruk Taban received his M.Sc. in Materials Engineering from the University of Southern California and Ph.D. in Mechanical Engineering from the University of Nevada. He has taught at the Western Nevada College and the University of Denver. Since 2017, he has been the Provost and VP for Academic Affairs at the North American University (NAU). In addition, Dr. Taban is currently serving as the Interim President of NAU.

Kamil Yildirim is a researcher at RUSMP at Rice University.

Yetkin Yildirim is the Director of STEM Projects at Rice University School Mathematics Project (RUSMP). Dr. Yildirim received his Ph.D. in Civil Engineering in 2000 from the University of Texas at Austin and is registered by the State of Texas as a professional engineer. Dr. Yildirim served as the president of Harmony Public Schools between 1999 and 2002. Dr. Yildirim has also served as the Director of the Engineering Education Research Center (EERC), where he conducted research regarding new developments in model-based learning activities in Science, Technology, Engineering, and Math (STEM) education. He served as the director of the Texas Pavement Preservation Center (TPPC) at the University of Texas at Austin for ten years and served as a faculty member in the civil engineering department at UT Austin for fifteen years.
